One of the apprentices, Joseph Holdham, left our employment on account of misbehaviour, about a week after George Mellor was committed to York. I saw Mellor in the afternoon of the day of the murder, about a quarter past six o’clock; there was a gentleman with him, whom I have not seen since. They came from the workshop into the house. George asked if my husband was in. I told him he was at the market. He then asked me if we wanted a man to work. I told him we had 110 occasion. He asked me to lend him a handkerchief, and I lent him a blaok silk one. He asked me if I would allow that gentleman to wash himself. He had not then a great coat on, he had put it off. The other person had a great coat—George asked to borrow a coat, and I told him my master’s coat was in the shop. They stopped about! a quarter-of-an-hour.

被告席上的囚犯之一乔治·梅勒的堂兄、呢绒修整工约瑟夫·梅勒的妻子玛莎·梅勒说：“我们住在地牢底，距离地牢林约两百码。当晚八九点钟之间，我第一次听说霍斯福尔先生遭枪击的事。我们家当时只有我们、一个孩子、四个学徒和一个女佣。在乔治·梅勒被押送至约克后大约一周，其中一名学徒约瑟夫·奥尔德姆因品行不端离职。在案发当天的下午六点一刻左右，我见到了梅勒，当时还有一位先生和他同行，此后我再也没见过那个人。他们从作坊走进屋里。乔治问我丈夫是否在家里。我告诉他他去赶集了。接着他问我我们是否需要招一个工人。我告诉他我们不需要。他问我借一块手帕，我借给他一块黑色的丝手帕。他问我是否可以让那位随行的先生洗把脸。他当时没穿外套，他把外套脱掉了。另一个人穿着大衣——乔治提出想借一件外套，我告诉他我丈夫的外套在作坊里。他们停留了大约一刻钟。”

Cross-examined by Mr. Hullock: Did not know where the apprentice was that ran away; he stopped with his father a few days. Fixed the time by the return of her husband, which was near seven o’clock. Mellor has always borne a good character since she knew him, which was about two years.

赫洛克先生对其进行了盘问：证人不知道那个跑掉的学徒去了哪里；他在他父亲那儿住了几天。她根据丈夫回来的时间确定了当时的时间，当时接近七点。自她认识梅勒以来（大约两年），他的品行一直很好。

Thomas Durrance, apprentice of Joseph Mellor: Is about 17 years old, was with Mellor in April last. Does not know George Mellor, but thinks he saw him at his master’s house, in the shop, on the night of the murder. There was a man with him; thinks Thorpe was with him. They had dark coloured coats when they came into the shop, he thinks topcoats. George Mellor took off his topcoat, and had then an under-coat, the topcoat he put on the brushing stone. Witness went upstairs with George, the other man did not go; when he got upstairs he gave him two pistols, about a foot long, they both put them under the flocks which are refuse of the cloth; this was a room where they work, there was not many flooks but sufficient to hide the pistols. Did not observe in what state the pistols were; did not know whether the trigger was up. Mellor said he need not say anything about them, but witness told his master when he oame home. Soon after they had hid the pistols, the persons left off work. Kinder, Joseph Holdham and Francis Vickerman were his fellow apprentices, he did not observe whether the pistols were discharged or not. His master and he hid them in the barn, and put straw upon them. He saw Mellor before Justice Jtiaddiffe, and saw him afterwards, when Mellor told him to mind and speak the truth about what he said and what he had seen. Mellor gave him five shillings and told him to give half to his fellow apprentice, Kinder, and not to say anything about the pistols.

约瑟夫·梅勒的学徒托马斯·杜兰斯说：他大约17岁，去年四月随梅勒工作。他不认识乔治·梅勒，但认为在案发当晚，在师傅家的作坊里见过他。当时还有一个人和他在一起，他认为那是索普。他们进作坊时穿着深色外套，他想大概是大衣。乔治·梅勒脱下了他的大衣，露出了里面的里衣，他把大衣搭在了刷呢石台面。证人随乔治一起上了楼，另一个人没去。上楼后，乔治给了他两把大约一英尺长的手枪，他们一起把手枪藏在剪呢碎末下面；那是一个他们干活的房间，碎呢不多，但足够用来藏枪。他没有注意手枪当时处于什么状态，也不知道板机是不是扣上的。梅勒告诉他不用提这事，但证人在师傅回家后便将此事告诉了师傅。他们把枪藏好后不久，大家就收工了。金德、约瑟夫·奥尔德姆和弗朗西斯·维克曼是他的同屋学徒，他没有注意手枪是否开过火。他和师傅一起把手枪藏到了谷仓里，并在上面盖上了麦草。他在治安官拉德克利夫面前见过梅勒，后来又见到了他，梅勒要他留意，并就自己所说和所见之事实话实说。梅勒给了他五先令，要他把一半分给他的师兄弟金德，并且不要透露关于手枪的任何事情。

Gross,examined by Mr Williams: Had never seen Mellor before that afternoon, and he was with him only a short time.

威廉姆斯先生对其进行了盘问：在那天下午之前他从未见过梅勒，且那天下午与他在一起的时间也很短。

Judge: “What time did you and your master take the pistols into the barn?”

Durrance: “In about two hours after they were put in the flocks.”

Judge: “What time did your master return home after those people were in your shop?”

Durrance: “In an hour-and-a-half.”

法官：“你和你师傅是什么时候把手枪拿到谷仓去的？”

杜兰斯：“在枪被放进碎呢里大约两小时后。”

法官：“在那些人来到你们作坊后，你师傅过了多久回到家的？”

杜兰斯：“一个半小时。”

John Kinder, apprentice to Joseph Mellor, upwards of 18 years of age, says:—He had not known Mellor a year, and did not know him right when Mr. Horsfall was shot; told Durrance he thought it was George Mellor; met him coming down from the flooks room that evening; he went into the room and Durrance showed him two pistols which he took from under the flocks. Does not know whether they were loaded, but thinks he blew through one of the touch holes and found it empty, he observed that it was not primed. He had half-a-crown from Durrance.

约瑟夫·梅勒的学徒约翰·金德，18岁以上，说：“他认识梅勒还不到一年，在霍斯福尔先生遇害时，他还不怎么能认清他；他曾对杜兰斯说他觉得那个人是乔治·梅勒；那天晚上他碰到梅勒正从放碎呢的房间走下来。他走进房间，杜兰斯向他展示了两把手枪，那是杜兰斯从碎呢堆下拿出来的。不知道手枪有没有装弹，但他认为自己曾对着其中一把枪的传火孔吹过气，发现里面是空的，他注意到那把枪没有装引火药。杜兰斯给了他半克朗。”

Mr. Joseph Mellor, oloth dresser, cousin to George Mellor:—Remembers the time when Mr. Horsfall was shot; witness left Huddersfield that evening at six and got home about seven o’clock. Durrance showed him two pistols that night; had not then heard that Horsfall was shot, but had seen a bust! among the military. Durrance and he went to hide the pistols in the laith under some straw. They did this for fear they should be found on his premises one of the pistols had a larger bore than the other He had heard Mellor say he had brought a pistol from Russia. One of them was without ramrod He found on his return home a dark topcoat on tl brushing stone with two cartridge balls in the pockets, he also found another dark green topcoat next morning; neither of these coats belonged to him. James Varley came on the Sunday after and he told him where the pistols were.”

被告乔治·梅勒的堂兄、呢绒修整工约瑟夫·梅勒先生说：“我记得霍斯福尔先生中枪的时间；我当晚六点离开哈德斯菲尔德，大约七点回到家。杜兰斯那天晚上向我展示了两把手枪。我当时还没有听说霍斯福尔被枪击的消息，但看到军队有些异常的骚动。杜兰斯和我一起去把手枪藏在谷仓的麦草下。我们这么做是怕在我的地方被搜出这枪。其中一把手枪的口径比另一把大。我曾听梅勒说过他从俄罗斯带回了一把手枪。其中一把枪没有通条。我回家时在刷呢石台上发现了一件深色大衣，口袋里有两个弹药球，第二天早上我还发现了一件墨绿色大衣。这两件大衣都不属于我。案发后的那个星期天，詹姆斯·瓦利来了，我告诉了他手枪藏在哪里。”

Judge: “Was there any coat of yours missing which you had left at home?”

Mellor: “Yes, a light drab topooat.”

法官：“你留在家里的大衣有丢的吗？”

梅勒：“有，一件浅褐色的外套。”

Mr. Staveley, the gaoler at the Castle produced a dark bottle green coat, which he said he took from Mellor soon after he came into the castle; and Joseph Mellor, being again called and asked to look at that coat, said it was like the coat he found on the brushing stone, but he could not swear to it.

城堡监狱的狱警斯塔夫利先生出示了一件深墨绿色的外套，他说这是在梅勒进入城堡后不久从他身上脱下来的。约瑟夫·梅勒再次被传唤并要求辨认这件大衣，他说这件大衣很像他在刷呢石台上发现的那件，但他无法宣誓确认。

Abraham Willie, a workman to Mr. Radcliffe, was in a building attending his horses that evening in the first olose beyond the plantation. He did not see any man in the wood, but saw four persons run down the plantation towards Dungeon Wood. They were within fifty yards of him. They all wore dark ooloured clothes. They were out of sight when they got a field length off. He heard that Mr. Horsfall was shot about three*or*four minutes after from two of his boys who were getting dung on the road.

拉德克利夫先生雇佣的工人亚伯拉罕·威利说，案发当晚他正在林地外的第一个马圈里照看马匹。他没有看到林子里有任何人，但看见四个人沿着林地朝地牢林跑去。他们距离他不到五十码。他们都穿着深色衣服。当他们跑过一片田野的距离后，就消失在视线之外了。大约三四分钟后，他从他那两个在路上捡粪的儿子那里听说霍斯福尔先生中枪了。

Edward Hartley was coming from Lockwood the evening Horfall was shot; was near the spot and heard a report of a gun, and he saw soon after four men run out of Mr. Radoliffe’s field, and jump over a wall in the direction of Dungeon Wood, they were all dressed in dark clothes; he saw the brass end of a pistol from under the coat of one of them; witness made an observation about seeing the pistol to a person who was with him, which he supposes must have been heard, as the man who held it immediately covered it with his top-coat.

爱德华·哈特利在霍斯福尔中枪当晚正从洛克伍德走来；他当时就在现场附近，听到了枪响，不久便看见四个男人从拉德克利夫先生的田里跑出来，翻过一堵墙朝地牢林方向跑去。他们都穿着深色衣服。他看见其中一个人大衣下露出了手枪的黄铜尾端。证人对身边的同伴低声说自己看见了手枪，他认为拿枪的人一定听到了这话，因为那人立刻用大衣把枪裹紧了。

Mary, the wife of Robert Robinson, publican, at Honley, heard of Mr. Horsfall’s murder the day it happened. Two young men came to their house that night and had something to drink. She remembers a collier was present, and one of the young men whistled very much and well, and the collier danced. The news of the murder was brought in soon after the young men entered, and she thought they looked down when they heard of it. They came between seven and eight and went away about nine. Her husband asked them where they came from, and they said from Longroyd Bridge.

洪利镇客栈老板罗伯特·罗宾逊的妻子玛丽说，她在案发当天就听听说霍斯福尔先生遇害的事。当晚有两个年轻人来到他们店里喝酒。她记得当时有一名煤矿工人在场，其中一个年轻人吹口哨吹得特别多、特别好，煤矿工人便跟着跳起舞来。那两个年轻人进来后不久，谋杀的消息就传开了，她觉得他们听到消息时神情显得有些沮丧。他们是在七八点钟之间来的，九点左右离开的。她丈夫问他们是从哪里来的，他们说是从龙罗伊德桥来的。

The case for the prosecution being now finished, Mr. Justice Le Blanc looked at the prisoners an instant in silence ,and then said

“This is the time, prisoners, to make your defence. Would you George Mellor, William Thorpe and Thomas Smith, wish to say anything for yourselves?”

Prisoners: We leave it to our Counsel.

控方举证至此全部结束，勒布兰克法官默默地注视了被告们片刻，然后说道：
“被告们，现在轮到你们进行辩护了。乔治·梅勒、威廉·索普、托马斯·史密斯，你们有什么要为自己申辩的吗？”
被告：“我们交由我们的辩护律师处理。”

### The Defence

### 辩护

William Hanson, the first witness called 011 behalf of the prisoners, said that he was at Huddersfield on the 28th of April, saw Mellor about a quarter of a mile from Huddersfield, going to Longroyd Bridge, about a quarter before seven.

代表被告方传唤的第一位证人威廉·汉森说，4月28日他在哈德斯菲尔德。下午差一刻七点左右，他看到梅勒在距离哈德斯菲尔德约四分之一英里的路上，正朝龙罗伊德桥走去。

John Womersley is a clock and watch maker, saw Mellor the evening Mr. Horsfall was shot, at a quarter after six in Huddersfield, at the corner of Cloth Hall-street, had a note in his pocket for him he owing him seven shillings for work done; went with him to Mr. Tavenor’s, the White Hart, near the Cloth Hall, and stopped in the house about twenty minutes, where he drank with the prisoner, Mellor, and left him there with one William Battersby. Witness then went to the Brown Cow, another public-house, and he had no sooner got in than the news arrived that Mr. Horsfall was shot, and the soldiers were going.

钟表匠约翰·沃默斯利说，在霍斯福尔先生遇害当晚六点一刻，他在哈德斯菲尔德呢绒大厅街转角处见到了梅勒。他口袋里有一张给梅勒的便条，梅勒欠他七先令工钱。他与梅勒一起去了呢绒大厅附近的塔文纳先生开的白鹿客栈，在店里呆了大约二十分钟。他在那里与被告梅勒一起喝酒，并留梅勒与一个叫威廉·巴特斯比的人在那里。证人随后去了另一家名叫棕牛的客栈，刚进店，霍斯福尔先生中枪、士兵正赶去现场的消息就传到了。

Cross-exainined by Mr. Park: Had just time to go to the Brown Cow after he had left the prisoner at Tavenor’s, it was about twenty minutes to seven o’olook, the prisoner paid him the seven shillings and he could produce the note; had no particular acquaintance with him, uut bad done work for his father for many years. From Longroyd Bridge to Huddersfield is about a quarter of a mile.

帕克先生对其进行了盘问：他离开在塔文纳客栈的被告后，刚好有足够的时间走到棕牛客栈，当时大约是差二十分七点。被告付给了他七先令，他能出示欠条；他与被告并无深交，但多年来一直帮他继父干活。从龙罗伊德桥到哈德斯菲尔德大约是四分之一英里。

Re-examined: Crosland Moor is more than twice as far from Huddersfield as Longroyd Bridge.

复问：克罗斯兰摩尔到哈德斯菲尔德的距离，是龙罗伊德桥到哈德斯菲尔德的两倍多。

William Battersby, the next witness, lived then at Taylor Hill; and recollects that evening by being at Tavenor’s, saw George Mellor and Jonathan Womersley, and drank with them; they had two pints of ale, and Jonathan Womersley left him in company with George Mellor, they were at this inn half-an-hour. At the end of that time they heard of Mr. Horsfall being shot for the first time. They came out and he parted with Mellor at the door.

下一位证人威廉·巴特斯比当时住在泰勒山；他记得那天晚上他也在塔文纳客栈，见到了乔治·梅勒和乔纳森·沃默斯利并与他们一同喝酒。他们喝了两品脱麦芽酒，乔纳森·沃默斯利离开后留下了他和乔治·梅勒。他们在客栈里呆了半小时。在最后，他们第一次听说霍斯福尔先生中枪的消息。他们走出来，他在门口与梅勒分手。

John Thorpe, lives at Castle Street, Huddersfield; has known Mellor sixteen or seventeen years; saw Mellor in Huddersfield, near the George Inn, on the evening Horsfall was shot, at ten minutes before six, witness had a watch he wanted to sell him, and stopped with him two or three minutes; he asked him if he would buy his watch, and produced it, when it appeared to be the time mentioned.

约翰·索普住在哈德斯菲尔德城堡街；他认识梅勒已有十六七年；在霍斯福尔遇害当晚五点五十分，他在哈德斯菲尔德的乔治客栈附近见到了梅勒。证人有一块表想卖给他，并和他停留了两三分钟；他问梅勒是否愿意买表并拿了出来，当时手表上显示的时间正是差十分六点。

Cross-examined by Mr. Topping: Does not know what street in Huddersfield the George Inn is in; says it wanted just ten minutes to six by his watch; Mellor examined the inside, and asked him the price of the watch; witness valued it at £3 13s., but he did not buy it.

托平先生对其进行了盘问：他不知道哈德斯菲尔德的乔治客栈在哪条街；他说当时自己的表上显示刚好差十分六点；梅勒检查了手表的内部结构并询问了价格；证人估价为3英镑13先令，但梅勒没有买。

Jonathan Battersby, a shoemaker, remembers the evening Mr. Horsfall was shot; he saw Mr. Horsfall that evening in his yard; he was on horseback, and went in the direction homeward.; it was between five and six o’clock; he went into his own house, stopped till he got his tea, and then put on his coat and went up the street, this was about twenty minutes after he had seen Mr. Horsfall ride up. He saw Mellor in the New Street, and conversed with him a minute or two. It was not quite six o’clock when he parted with the prisoner at the bar, he then went home, and heard of the shooting of Mr. Horsfall as soon as he got homa

鞋匠乔纳森·巴特斯比记得霍斯福尔先生中枪的当晚；那天傍晚他在他父亲的院子里见到了霍斯福尔先生。当时霍斯福尔骑着马朝回家的方向走去，时间在五点到六点之间。他进屋喝了茶，然后穿上外套走到街上，这距离他看到霍斯福尔先生骑马过去大约过了二十分钟。他在新街碰见梅勒，并和他交谈了一两分钟。当他与被告分手时还不到六点。随后他回了家，一到家就听说了霍斯福尔先生中枪的消息。

George Armitage, blacksmith, living at Lockwood, knows where Joseph Mellor lives, his house is between the Dungeon and Huddersfield, saw George Mellor come past between five and six o’clock, they go to their drinking sometimes at five, and sometimes at half-past. That evening he had been detained by a job till after five o’clock; had conversation with the prisoner; observed that he was coming from the bar, and going towards Huddersfield. He said he was coming from Joseph Mellor’s.

住在洛克伍德的铁匠乔治·阿米蒂奇知道约瑟夫·梅勒住在哪里（他的木屋位于地牢和哈德斯菲尔德之间）。在五点到六点之间，他看见乔治·梅勒走过去。他们有时在五点去喝茶，有时在五点半。那天傍晚他因为干一件活一直拖到了五点以后。他与被告交谈了几句；注意到被告是从路障关卡方向走来，正朝哈德斯菲尔德方向走去。被告说他是从约瑟夫·梅勒家来的。

Cross-examined by Mr. Park: Mellor said he had been at Joseph Mellor’s, with a man that wanted work.

帕克先生对其进行了盘问：梅勒说他和一个想找工作的人一起去过约瑟夫·梅勒家。

Joseph Armitage, saw Mr. Horsfall at his shop door, going towards Huddersfield from Lockwood Bar, saw his brother talking with George Mellor at drinking time, between five and six o’clock.

约瑟夫·阿米蒂奇说他在作坊门口看见霍斯福尔先生正从洛克伍德路障关卡朝哈德斯菲尔德方向走去。他看见他兄弟在喝茶时间与乔治·梅勒谈话。

Charles Ratcliffe, cloth dresser, of Huddersfield, was at Mr. Fisher’s, of Longroyd Bridge, on the afternoon of the day of Mr. Horsfall’s murder, seeking work; was in the raising shop at half-past five; lenows Thorpe, and saw him raising a blue coat piece In that room; he conversed with him for a quarter-of-an-hour or upwards; left him there, and there was a young woman fetching water in a can, out of the raising shop. He returned to Huddersfield, where he arrived twenty minutes after six, by the Cloth Hall dock; about half-an-hour after he heard of Mr .Horsfall being shot at.

哈德斯菲尔德的呢绒修整工查尔斯·拉德克利夫在霍斯福尔先生遇害那天下午前往龙罗伊德桥的费舍尔先生处找工作。五点半时他在拉绒作坊里；他认识索普，看见索普在那个房间里给一件蓝色外衣面料拉绒。他与索普交谈了一刻钟或更久，然后离开了他。当时有一位年轻女子正提着水罐从拉绒作坊里打水。他返回哈德斯菲尔德，根据呢绒大厅的大钟显示，他到达的时间是六点二十分；大约半小时后，他听说了霍斯福尔先生遭枪击的消息。

Cross-examined by Mr. Topping: He was in search of work, did not see Mr. Fisher; Thorpe was the only man he saw or conversed with there.

托平先生对其进行了盘问：他是去那里找工作的，没有见到费舍尔先生；索普是他当时在那里唯一见到并交谈过的人。

Frances Mid wood, of Longroyd Bridge, keeps her father’s house, and says that on the 23th of April, as their usual practice is, they drank tea about halfpast four o’clock. The next day was their washing day, and they got water from Mr. Fisher’s shop. About five o’clock, just after tea, she went with a (sin to fetch water from that shop. The first time she went there was nobody in the shop; it requires about ten minutes to go, and she returned immediately, and then saw William Thorpe. He asked her if she was fetching water, and she said “Yes.” Went the third time, some other person was with him. She continued fetching water for some time. Saw W. Thorpe every time she went for water except the first. One of the times she saw Abraham Pilling, a shoemaker, who was bringing her a pair of new shoes. He followed her into the shop and she left him with Thorpe.

来自龙罗伊德桥的弗朗西丝·米德伍德照看她父亲的家。她说，在4月28日，他们像往常一样在下午四点半左右喝了茶。第二天是他们的洗衣日，他们需要从费舍尔先生的作坊接水。大约五点钟，喝完茶后不久，她拿着水罐去那个作坊打水。第一趟去时作坊里空无一人。去一趟需要大约十分钟，她立即返回，然后看见了威廉·索普。索普问她是否是来打水的，她说“是的”。她去第三趟时，有另外一个人和索普在一起。她连续去打水打了一段时间。除了第一趟，每次去打水她都能见到威廉·索普。其中有一次她见到了鞋匠亚伯拉罕·皮林，他是来给她送一双新鞋的。皮林跟着她进了作坊，她把皮林和索普留在了一起。

Cross-examined by Mr. Park: She does not know how long it takes to go from Joseph Mellor’s to Fisher’s, nor does she recolleot when she was first asked at what hour they drank tea on the 29th of April; it was not within these few days. When she was asked it was in the presence of Mr Blackburn, the prisoner’s attorney. A person fetched her to Mr. Blackburn’s, but that person did not put the question to her; she first told them soon after the prisoners came to York, but she cannot recolleot the exact time.

帕克先生对其进行了盘问：她不知道从约瑟夫·梅勒家到费舍尔家需要走多久，也不记得是在什么时候第一次被问及4月28日喝茶的时间的；那绝不是这几天里的事。当她被询问时，被告的代理律师黑尔斯本先生在场。是有人把她带到黑尔斯本先生那里的，但那个人并没有问她问题。她是在被告押往约克后不久第一次告诉他们的，但她记不清确切的时间了。

Abraham Pilling, shoemaker, made the last witnees’s shoes and delivered them on the night the murder was oommitted; he took them from Huddersfield to her father’s and he saw her crossing the road with a can in her hand. He followed her to the door which goes into the raising house; found Fanny Midwood and William Thorpe there, she was lading water out of a cistern with a can. He waited till she had done. It was a quarter to six when he set off home, and his house is a mile from Longroyd Bridge. She asked the price of the shoes, and went and fetched a guinea note belonging to Ingham’s bank. He continued in the place and had some discourse with Thorpe. He stopped in the place about half-an-hour, when he set out for the Mar&h, which is nearly another mile on the Lindlev Road, but when he came out of the raising shop and had just got into the lane, he was told that Mr. Harsfall was shot.

鞋匠亚伯拉罕·皮林说，他做好了弗朗西丝的鞋子，并在案发当晚送了过去。他把鞋从哈德斯菲尔德送到她父亲家，看见她手里提着水罐正穿过马路。他跟着她走到通往拉绒室的门口，发现芬妮·米德伍德和威廉·索普在里面，她正用罐子从水槽里舀水。他一直等到她舀完水。他动身回家时是差一刻六点，他家距离龙罗伊德桥有一英里。她询问了鞋的价格，接着回屋拿了一张英格姆银行的一金尼钞票。他继续留在作坊里，并与索普交谈了一会儿。他在那里呆了大约半小时，然后启程前往马什（这地方距离林德利路有将近另一英里远）。然而，当他走出拉绒室刚刚进巷子时，就被告知霍斯福尔先生中枪了。

John Bower, a boy, about seventeen, apprentice to Mr. Wood, of Longroyd Bridge, examined by Mr. Williams said,—Mellor superintends the work for Mr. Wood; reoollects the day Mr. Horsfall was shot at; saw Mellor that day in the afternoon; they were pressing. The press was to harden, and Mellor assisted at hardening the press; there were Thomas Smith, Benjamin Walker, James Varley, and John Walker; it was near seven o’clook; is quite sure Benjamin Walker and Smith were present at the hardening of the press; Smith, the prisoner, was an apprentice. William Hall and he slept in the same room.

约十七岁的男孩、龙罗伊德桥伍德先生的学徒约翰·鲍尔接受威廉姆斯先生审问时说：梅勒负责监管伍德先生工场的活；他记得霍斯福尔先生遭枪击的那天；那天下午他见到了梅勒，他们当时正在压呢。当时需要将压呢机固定，梅勒协助完成了固定工作。当时在场的有托马斯·史密斯、本杰明·沃克、詹姆斯·瓦利和约翰·沃克。时间接近七点；他确信在固定压呢机时本杰明·沃克和史密斯都在场。被告史密斯是个学徒。威廉·霍尔和他睡在同一个房间。

William Hall says: Widow Hartley brought the news about seven o’clook, it was a particularly busy day, and they did not go to drinking till six o’clock, and soon after they heard that Mr. Horsfall was shot.

威廉·霍尔说：寡妇哈特利大约在七点钟送来了消息，那天特别忙，他们直到六点钟才去喝茶，不久之后他们就听说霍斯福尔先生中枪了。

William Hirst, of Longroyd Bridge, has lodged at Mr. Wood’s some years. Remembers Benjamin Walker; remembers the time when Mr. Horsfall was shot, there was noise enough about that; when he first heard of it, he said to Benjamin Walker, “Horsfall iB shot!” and Walker replied, “That is too good news to be true.”

龙罗伊德桥的威廉·赫斯特已在伍德先生家借宿了几年。他记得本杰明·沃克。他记得霍斯福尔先生中枪的时候，当时这事闹得满城风雨；当他第一次听说这事时，他对本杰明·沃克说：“霍斯福尔中枪了！”沃克回答道：“这消息太好以至于不可能是真的。”

It was objected by the Counsel of the Crown, that what Walker said was not evidence and ought not to be received, and in that objection his Lordship concurred. William Hirst, in continuation, said he had been at Hliddersfield that day, and left it about twenty minutes before seven o’clock.

控方律师提出反对，认为沃克所说的话不能作为证据，不予采信，法官大人赞同了这一反对意见。威廉·赫斯特继续陈述说，他那天去了哈德斯菲尔德，并在大约差二十分七点时离开。

Croes-examined by Mr. Topping: He was no relation to any of the prisoners; his son is a merchant in Huddersfield, and employs Mr. Wood as well as many other people. On being asked what he meant by saying that there was noise enough about the murder of Horsfall, he said all he meant was, that it was a very bad thing, and produced a great outcry.

托平先生对其进行了盘问：他与任何一个被告均无亲属关系；他的儿子是哈德斯菲尔德的商人，雇佣了伍德先生以及许多其他人。当被问及他说霍斯福尔被谋杀一事闹得满城风雨是什么意思时，他说他的意思只是这件事情非常恶劣，引起了巨大的轰动。

Joseph Rushworth, the last witness called, said he lived at the bottom of Cowoliffe, that he knows William Hall, who has been examined here to-day, remembers being before Mr. Radoliffe on the 12th of October; Hall was coming to Mr. Radcliffe’s when he saw him, but had no conversation with him after he had been before the Magistrate.

最后被传唤的证人约瑟夫·拉什沃斯说，他住在考克里夫山脚下，他认识今天在这里接受过质询的威廉·霍尔。他记得10月12日自己曾去拉德克利夫先生那里；当时他看见霍尔正朝拉德克利夫先生家走去，但在霍尔接受治安官讯问后，他没有与他有过交谈。
<!-- Part 035 end -->

<!-- Part 036 start -->
## Chapter XXV Execution of Mellor, Thorpe, and Smith.

第二十五章 处决梅勒、索普和史密斯

“Let no one trust the first false step

Of guilt, it hangs upon a precipice

Whose steep descent in sure perdition ends.”

“切莫信赖罪恶的第一步错，
它悬于悬崖峭壁之上，
那陡峭的下坡终结于万劫不复。”

> *-- —*Young.**
> *——杨格*

The witnesses now having all been examined Mr. Justioe Le Blano proceeded to sum up, and in doing so recapitulated the wnole of the evidence. He went through that for the defence with special minuteness and pointed out the conflicting statements made with respect to the time the various witnesses asserted they saw prisoners, concluding his long address as follows:—”This, gentlemen is the evidence produced on the part of the prisoners; evidence of different persons ooming in at different times, far the purpose of shewing that, at points of time on that evening which they fix, they saw either all the prisoners, or some of them, in situations not consistent with the time allotted by the account given by the other witnesses for this transaction. When you see the point of time at which it took place, and the nearness of all the different spots where they were to the place in question, perhaps it is not so surprising that there should, at such a distance of time, be so much variance in the account given by different witnesses as to the periods of the day, as there appears to be upon the occasion. Even supposing the witnesses to come under no improper bias or influence in what they are saying, they are speaking of a transaction which not only took place a long time ago, but was not imputed to the prisoners at the bar till a considerable time after it had taken place. For this happened in the month of April, and it does not appear that enquiry was made before a magistrate, or any of these persons committed, till the month of October. Nothing happened immediately after the transaction, to lead these persons particularly to watch, so as to be accurate in the hour or time on the particular evening, when they saw these persons at a particular place; and then we know how persons are apt to be mistaken, even when care is taken in point of time. However, gentlemen, it is for you to compare the evidence.

证人质询至此已全部结束，勒布兰克法官开始进行总结，并在总结中概述了全部证据。他以极尽详尽的态度梳理了辩方的证据，并指出了不同证人在声称看见被告的时间上存在的矛盾陈述。他长篇发言的结语如下：“诸位，以上即是为被告们提供的证言；不同证人在不同时间出面作证，旨在表明在他们所确定的当晚某些时间点上，他们见到了全部或部分被告，而这些情况与控方证人就本案交易时间所作的描述是不相符的。考虑到案发的时间点，以及他们所在的各个不同地点与涉案地点的接近程度，在时隔这么久之后，不同证人对当天时间段的叙述出现如此多的差异，或许也就不足为奇了。即使假设证人们在陈述时没有受到任何不当偏见或影响，但他们所说的是一件不仅发生在上演已久的旧事，而且直到案发相当长一段时间后才归罪于被告席上的囚犯。因为这发生在四月份，而直到十月份才在治安官面前进行调查，或将这些人收监。案发后并没有立即发生任何事情促使这些人特别去留意，以便能精确记住那个特定夜晚他们在特定地点见到这些人的具体钟点或时间；我们也知道，即使在注意时间的情况下，人们也难免会犯错。然而，诸位，比较证据是你们的职责。

The evidence on the part of the prosecution rests, not on the testimony of Benjamin Walker only, but of several other workmen in this manufactory, who are not accomplices in this transaction, though they appear to have had a knowledge of it, which I cannot say is not to a certain degree guilty. For I cannot hold them innocent in knowing of such a transaction going on, and treating it so lightly as to give no information respecting it, and to keep it concealed longer than while there was an immediate impression of fear of personal danger to themselves. But, independently of this, you have evidence of that which appears to me to be the strongest part of the case, and requires the most explanation, but which has not been explained, and which applies particularly to the prisoner Mellor, and to Thorpe, if you are satisfied he was with Mellor; I mean the transaction which took place at Joseph Mellor s house at Dungeon Wood, and which goes to contradict, in point of time, the evidence given by the different witnesses on the part of the defendants, llie enquiry is a serious one, not only as regards the prisoners themselves, but as regards the public peace and security. You, who have heard the evidence, which lay the facts together in your minds, will do justice between the country and the prisoners.”

“控方的证据不仅依赖于本杰明·沃克的证词，还依赖于该工场其他几名工人的证词。他们并不是这起案件的共犯，尽管他们似乎知情，但我不能说他们在某种程度上是无罪的。因为他们既然知道有这种事情在进行，却视若无睹，不提供任何相关线索，并且除了出于自身人身安全的直接恐惧之外，还长期对此予以隐瞒，因此我不能认为他们是清白的。但除此以外，你们还拥有在我看来是本案最有力、最需要解释但却未被解释的证据，这尤其适用于被告梅勒，以及索普（如果你们确信他当时与梅勒在一起的话）；我指的是在地下林约瑟夫·梅勒家发生的那次交易，它在时间上驳斥了被告方不同证人所提供的证言。这次审判是严肃的，不仅关乎囚犯们本身，也关乎公众的和平与安全。听过证言并已在脑海中将事实整理清楚的诸位，将在国家与囚犯之间做出公正的裁决。”

The jury retired at half-past seven, and returned five minutes before eight, finding,

陪审团于七点半退庭商议，并在八点差五分时返回，裁定：

> GEORGE MELLOR, GUILTY.
> WILLIAM THORPE, GUILTY.
> THOMAS SMITH, GUILTY.

> 乔治·梅勒，有罪。
> 威廉·索普，有罪。
> 托马斯·史密斯，有罪。

The prisoners being severally asked in the usual manner, by the Clerk of Arraigns, if they had anything to say, why sentence of death should not be pronounced upon them, answered:

起诉书记官随后按照惯例依次询问被告，是否有任何理由说明法庭不应对他们宣判死刑，被告答道：

Mellor.—“I have nothing to say, only that I am not guilty.”

梅勒：“我没什么好说的，只是一点，我是无罪的。”

Thorpe.—“I am not guilty, sir; evidence has been given false against me: That I declare.”

索普：“我是无罪的，先生；有人提供了针对我的伪证：我对此予以声明。”

Smith.—“Not guilty, sir.”

史密斯：“无罪，先生。”

### The Sentence

### 判决

Mr. Justice Le Blanc immediately passed sentence of death upon them in the following words:

勒布兰克法官随即用以下词句对他们宣判了死刑：

“You, the several prisoners at the bar, have been tried and convicted of wilful and deliberate murder —under all circumstances an offence of the deepest malignity, but under the circumstances which have appeared in this case in particular, as far as one crime of the same denomination can be distinguished from another, this may be pronounced a crime of the blackest dye. In other cases, the court has been able to discover something which might work upon the passions of mankind, and induce them to commit an act, at whch in their cooler moments their minds would have revolted, but, in the present case, the crime was committed against a man who appears to have given no offence to any one of you, except that he was suspected of having expressed himself with a manly feeling against those who had set up a right to violate all property, and to take away the life of any man who had been supposed to encourage others to do, (what I trust there still are men sufficient in the country to do,) to stand manfully forward in defence of their property. For that reason, he was marked out by you as an object of the most cowardly revenge. You, attempting to associate with yourselves such men as you could prevail upon to join in your wicked purposes, waylay him at the moment when he is returining home, almost in midday with a boldness which one had scaroely ever witnessed in trying offences of this description. But in the course of your trial, proceedings have come before the court, at which human nature shudders. That the national character should be so debased; that men, who ought to boast of their character as Britons should have dared to hold forth, in the language which you have held forth, and with so little discretion, assassination and destruction of property were instruments in your hands, to be exercised at your pleasure, and against any person who had happened to offend you—independently of this, that you should have dared to take into your hands the Holy Scriptures, and to administer an impious oath to those who were cognisant of your offence, calling the Almighty as a witness, (that Being whom you were conscious you had offended in the highest degree;) calling upon Him for vengeance upon the heads of those who should discover your crimes; these are oircumstances which have appeared in the course of this trial, and which have scarcely ever appeared in the course of any trial which has been brought before a court of justice.

“你们，被告席上的几位囚犯，已经因蓄意谋杀罪接受了审判并被判有罪——在任何情况下，这都是性质极其恶劣的罪行。但在本案所呈现的特定情况下，就同类罪行彼此之间的区别而言，这可以说是最黑暗、最不可饶恕的罪行。在其他案件中，法庭或许还能发现某些触动人类情感的因素，促使他们做出在冷静时会感到厌恶的举动。然而在审理本案时，这起罪行所针对的却是一个似乎从未冒犯过你们任何人的绅士，唯一的借口就是他被怀疑曾以大无畏的立场，公开反对那些声称有权侵犯一切财产、并企图剥夺任何涉嫌鼓励他人（我相信本国仍有足够的人愿意这样做）挺身而出捍卫自己财产之人的生命。由于这个原因，他被你们视作卑鄙报复的目标。你们企图纠集那些能被你们说服并加入你们邪恶企图的人，在他回家的途中设伏，且几乎是在光天化日之下，其胆大妄为之程度在审理此类案件中实属罕见。但在审判过程中，法庭所了解到的情节令人类本性为之战栗。国民性格竟堕落至此；本应为不列颠人性格自豪的人，竟敢用你们所使用的语言毫无顾忌地宣称，暗杀和破坏财产是你们手中的工具，可凭你们的喜好任意施加在任何碰巧冒犯了你们的人身上——不仅如此，你们竟然还敢手捧神圣的经书，向那些对你们的罪行心知肚明的人主持不敬的宣誓，呼唤全能者作证（而你们明明深知自己已经极大地冒犯了祂）；呼唤祂对那些可能揭发你们罪行的人降下惩罚。这些在庭审过程中浮现的情节，在提交法庭审理的任何案件中都几乎闻所未闻。

“It is not upon the testimony of one, or two, or of three witnesses, that your guilt depends; and let me advise you not to lay that balm to your souls, that you have been deprived by false accusation, and by false oaths, of your lives. A chain of circumstances has been discovered in the course of this trial, which does not depend upon the oath of any one, or two, or three men, whom you may denominate even as bad as yourselves. But even from the testimony of those, who, if they had not been honest to a certain degree, would have’ given a different evidence, it is clear that two at least of you were guilty; and as little doubt remains, from other Evidence, upon the guilt of the third of you.

“你们的有罪判定并非仅仅依赖于一两个或三个证人的证言；让我奉劝你们，不要用‘自己是因为虚假的指控和伪造的誓言而被夺去生命’这样的谎言来慰藉你们的灵魂。在审理过程中发现的一系列环环相扣的事实，并不依赖于任何一两个或三个甚至被你们称作与你们同样坏的人的誓言。然而，即使是从那些如果不是在某种程度上保持诚实就会给出截然不同证言的人的证词中，也显而易见你们中至少有两人是有罪的；而且根据其他证据，第三个人的罪行也同样毋庸置疑。

‘ In the shop where you have worked, some of you appear to have gained such an ascendency over the minds and over the consciences of the workmen, who were in some degree under your control, that you could mould and fashion them to any wicked purpose you yourselves might imagine. Their eyes, I hope, will be opened by the fate which awaits you; they will see, that though for a short time the career of the wicked may continue, yet the law is sure at length to overtake them.

“在你们工作的作坊里，你们中的某些人似乎对在一定程度上受你们控制的工人们的思想和良知获得了极大的支配权，以至于你们可以将他们塑造成你们所能想象到的任何邪恶用途。我希望，等待着你们的命运能擦亮他们的双眼；他们将会看到，尽管恶人的生涯可能会短暂持续，但法律最终必定会追上他们。

“To you, the unfortunate persons who stand at the bar, (for every man who has disgraced his character as you have must be deemed unfortunate,) to you the only kindness I can offer, is in the advice to prepare, as speedily as you can, for that execution of this sentence, which must shortly await you; to make the best you can of the period still allotted to you in this world—longer far than was allowed to the unfortunate person who was the object of your revenge; that you will take the opportunity of making your peace with the Almighty Being whom you have offended; that by the sincerity of your repentance, the fulness of your offenoes, you may endeavour to obtain forgiveness in the world to come which I oonnot hold out to you any hopes of obtaining in his worlds

“对于你们，站在被告席上的不幸之人（因为每一个像你们这样败坏了自己品格的人都必须被视为是不幸的），我所能提供的唯一善意就是奉劝你们尽快为即将执行的死刑做好准备；在你们仍被允许留在这个世界上的时间里做好最充分的准备——这段时间远比被你们报复的那个不幸之人所获得的时间长得多；你们应该抓住机会，与被你们冒犯的全能主实现和解；通过你们真诚的忏悔和对罪行的深切悔悟，努力在来世获得宽恕，因为我无法向你们提供在今生获得宽恕的任何希望。

“It remains only for me to pass upon you the sentence of the law. That sentence is—That you, the three prisoners at the bar, be taken from hence to the place from whence you came, and from thence, on Friday next, to the place of execution; that you be there hanged by the neck till you are dead, your bodies afterwards to be delivered to the surgeons to be dissected and anatomized, according to the directions of the statue. And may God have meroy upon your souls.”

“现在只剩下我对你们宣读法律的判决。这项判决是——你们，被告席上的三名囚犯，从这里被带回原收监处，并于下周五押解至刑场；在那里被绞住颈部直至死亡，随后你们的尸体将按照法规的指示移交给外科医生进行解剖和剖析。愿上帝怜悯你们的灵魂。”

The prisoners, who still retained their self-possession, were then removed from the bar, where they had stood from nine o’clook in the morning to nine o’clock at night. During the whole length of the trial, and even while the solemn sentence of the law was passed, not one of them shed a tear, but their behaviour was perfectly free from any indecent boldness or unbecoming levity. The proceedings of the court were conducted with unusual solemnities, and the behaviour of the spectators was strictly decorous and becoming. From amongst the numerous relatives and friends of the unfortunate malefactors, an expression of anguish frequently reached the ear, but it was deep, not loud; and in that part of the auditory that was connected with them, only by a common nature, abhorrence at their enormous crime was not unmixed with commiseration, at the premature fate of three victims of a lawless confederacy.

被告们依然保持着镇静，随后被带离了法庭，他们从早上九点一直站到晚上九点。在整个审判过程中，甚至在宣读严肃的法律判决时，他们中没有一个人流下一滴眼泪，但他们的举止绝无任何粗鄙的张狂或不体面的轻浮。法庭的诉讼程序在异乎寻常的庄严气氛中进行，旁听群众的举止也十分端庄得体。在不幸的犯人众多的亲友中，经常传来痛苦的叹息，但那声音低沉而非喧哗；而在那些仅因同为人类而与他们产生联系的旁听者中，对他们滔天罪行的痛恨也夹杂着同情，同情这三个不法联盟牺牲品英年早逝的命运。

So engrossed had Kay nor and Mary been in the trials that they did not know how time flew; and when as they left the crowded court the great Ixill overhead struck nine, they were greatly astonished. They made their way swiftly to their lodgings full of excitement at the events they had witnessed.

由于雷纳和玛丽全身心地投入到了庭审中，他们根本没有意识到时间飞逝；当他们离开拥挤的法庭时，头顶上的大钟敲响了九下，这让他们大吃一惊。他们满脑子都是刚才亲眼目睹的那些惊心动魄的场面，飞快地朝住处走去。

At the opening of the court cn Thursday morning, the jury recommended Thos. Smith to mercy, and an application was made to the Judges to have the sentence of the law, on such of the murderers as they might think proper to order for execution carried into effect not at the usual place of execution, but on the spot where the murder was perpetrated: but it was not thought expedient to comply with these applications.

在周四早上法庭开庭时，陪审团建议对托马斯·史密斯予以宽大处理。并且有人向法官提出申请，要求将那些被判处死刑的凶手的刑罚不是在通常的刑场执行，而是在谋杀案发生的地点执行，但法官认为采纳这些申请并不妥当。

### The Execution

### 处决

In the interval between the trial and the execution, the prisoners behaved very penitently, though they refused to make any confession either in the prison, or the place of execution. Thorpe, on being asked if he did not acknowledge the justice of the sentence said—”Do not ask me any questions.” Mellor declared—” That he would rather be in the situation he was then placed, dreadful as it was, than have to answer for the crime of their accuser, and that he would not change situations with him, even for his liberty and two thousand pounds;” but with all his resolution, he could not conceal the agonies of his mind,, for on the night before the execution he fell on the ground in a state of insensibility, and it was thought he would have died in his cell; but he slowly recovered, and in the morning his health seemed to be restored.

在审判与处决之间的这段时间里，囚犯们表现得很是忏悔，尽管他们拒绝在监狱或刑场做出任何交代。当被问及是否承认判决公允时，索普说：“不要问我任何问题。”梅勒宣称：“我宁愿处于我现在所处的境地，尽管它很可怕，也不愿去为我们控诉者的罪行负责，我绝不会为了自由和两千英镑而与他交换处境。”然而，尽管他意志坚强，却无法掩饰内心的极度痛苦，因为在行刑前一天晚上，他昏倒在地上失去了知觉，人们曾以为他会死在牢房里；但他慢慢醒了过来，到了早上身体似乎恢复了健康。

The execution of these unhappy men took place on Friday, January 8th, 1813, at nine o’clock, at the usual place behind the Castle, at York. Every precaution had been taken to render every idea of a rescue impracticable. Two troops of cavalry were drawn up in front of the drop, and the avenues to the castle were guarded by infantry. Five minutes before nine o’clock the prisoners came upon the fatal platform. After the ordinary had read the accustomed forms of prayer on these occasions, George Mello’r prayed for about ten minutes; he spoke with great fervency and devotion, confessing in general the greatness of liis sins, but without making any confession of the crime for which he suffered. He prayed earnestly for mercy, and with a pathos that was affecting. William Thorpe also prayed, but his voice was not so well heard. Smith said little, but seemed to join in the devotion with great seriousness. The prisoners were then moved to the front of the platform, and Mellor said—“Some of my enemies may be here, if there be, I freely forgive them, and all the world, and I hope the world will forgive me.” William Thorpe said—“I hope none of those who are now before me will ever come to this place.” The executioner then proceeded to perform his fatal office, and the drop fell. Some alteration had been made in the drop, so that the whole of the bodies were visible when they were suspended; in former executions only the feet and head could be seen by the spectators. They were executed in their irons. They appeared slightly convulsed for a few moments.

对这几名不幸之人的处决于1813年1月8日星期五早上九点，在约克城堡后面的常规刑场执行。法庭采取了极其严密的防范措施，使任何劫法场的企图都化为泡影。两队骑兵在绞刑架前严阵以待，通往城堡的所有通道都由步兵把守。八点五十五分，囚犯们走上了致命的绞刑台。在随监司铎宣读了此类场合惯用的祈祷词后，乔治·梅勒祈祷了大约十分钟；他说话非常虔诚挚热，大致忏悔了自己深重的罪孽，但没有对他因此受刑的罪行做出任何具体交待。他热切地祈求怜悯，其哀婉的语气令人动容。威廉·索普也进行了祈祷，但他的声音听得不太清楚。史密斯话不多，但似乎以极其严肃的态度加入了祈祷。囚犯们随后被带到绞刑台前部，梅勒说：“我的某些仇人可能会在这里，如果真在，我宽恕他们，也宽恕全世界，我希望世界也会宽恕我。”威廉·索普说：“我希望现在站在我面前的任何一个人都永远不要来到这个地方。”随后绞刑吏执行了死刑，绞架的底板落了下去。绞刑架做了一些改动，使他们悬挂时全身都可以被看见；在以前的处决中，观众只能看到脚和头部。他们是戴着脚镣被处决的。他们看起来微微抽搐了几秒钟。

The number of people assembled was much greater than is usual on these melancholy occasions, but not the slightest indication of tumult prevailed, and the greatest silence reigned during the whole of this solemn and painful scene. Such was the issue of that fatal system, which, after having produced in its progress great terror and alarm, and much mischief to the community, at length terminated in the death of those who were its most active partizans. And thus perished in the very bloom of life, three young men, who, had they directed their talents to lawful pursuits, might have lived happy and respected. They were young men on whose countenances nature had not imprinted the features of assassins.

聚集的人数比以往这类令人忧伤的场合要多得多，但现场没有出现一丝骚乱的迹象，在整个庄严而痛苦的场景中，大家都保持着极大的静默。这就是那个致命体系的结局——它在发展过程中曾给社会带来极大的恐慌、警报和许多危害，但最终以其最活跃的骨干分子的死亡而告终。就这样，三名正值青春年华的年轻人死去了。如果他们把自己的才华用在合法的追求上，他们本可以生活得幸福而受人尊敬。在这些年轻人的脸上，大自然并没有刻下刺客的特征。

## Chapter XXVI Trials of the Halifax Luddites, also of William Hartley and Others.

第二十六章 审判哈利法克斯的卢德分子，以及威廉·哈特利等人

“Quick with the tale and ready with the ie,

The genial confederate and general spy.”

“编故事敏捷，扯谎也快，
是和蔼的同伙，也是无孔不入的间谍。”

> *-- —*Byron,**
> *——拜伦*

“A hopeless darkness settles o’er my fate, My life is closed.”

“绝望的黑暗笼罩着我的命运，我的生命已然终结。”

> *-- —*Basil.**
> *——《巴席尔》*

The excitement of the eventful day proved too much for Mary Hartley. So long as her mind was occupied by watching the tragic proceedings she was not conscdous of her weakness, but when her lodgings were reached she fell down in a dead faint, and had to be carried to bed, where she passed the night in a state of fever, and when the morning dawned was utterly unable to rise.

这波折重重的一天带来的巨大刺激，让玛丽·哈特利难以承受。当她的注意力完全集中在观看那场悲惨的庭审程序上时，她并没有意识到自己的虚弱；然而一回到住处，她便一头栽倒，昏死过去，只得被抬上床。她在高烧中度过了这一夜，次日清晨来临时，已完全无法起床。

Leaving her in the care of the kind landlady, Rayner next day again made his way to the Castle. Again he was placed in a good seat by the custodian, and after several weary hours of waiting the court was opened in due form, and the Halifax Luddites were placed in the dock.

将她托付给好心的房东太太照顾后，雷纳第二天再次动身前往城堡。他再次被看守员安置在了一个好座位上。经过几个小时令人疲惫的等待后，法庭正式开庭，哈利法克斯的卢德分子被带上了被告席。

These were old John Baines (66), Charles Milnes (22), John Baines, the younger (34), William Blakeborough (22), Geo. Duckworth (23), and /aoharv Baines (15).

他们是：老约翰·贝恩斯（66岁）、查尔斯·米尔内斯（22岁）、小约翰·贝恩斯（34岁）、威廉·布莱克伯勒（22岁）、乔治·达克沃斯（23岁）以及扎卡里·贝恩斯（15岁）。

The prisoners were charged with administering an unlawful oath to John McDonald, on July 8th, 5812.

被告们被指控于1812年7月8日向约翰·麦克唐纳主持宣读了非法誓言。

The prosecuting counsel said that it appeared that in July last, John MoDonald and another spy were sent from Manchester (by Mr. Nadin, a very active police officer there) to Halifax, to discover offenders of the description charged in the indictment, and they arrived there on the 8th of July. They went to a publichouse called the Crispin; there they dined, and afterwards went to look for lodgings and then returned. The circumstances of the attack on Cartwright’s mill were talked about at the Crispin. The prisoner Milnes was there, and M’Donald entered into conversation with him about the hardness of the times. In the course of the conversation about the mill, Milnes said he knew the two men who were killed there very well, and spoke of the activity of the person who commanded as an officer. M’Donald said he would be as active as any of them if they would twist him in, or swear him in. Milnes said he had got many blank cartridges from a soldier, that he had been searched for, but he got away and stayed away three weeks. Milnes told M’Donald he could get him sworn in by an old man who lived near. M’Donald said he was willing. They sat wgether till it was dark, and then went together to Baines’s house about ten at night; all the prisoners were there. Milnes told them that he (M’Donald) was a stranger, but a good fellow, and wished to be a brother. Old Baines then said, he must be handy about it, because he expected the watch and ward in before eleven o’clock. He said nothing more, but got a paper and a book about the size of a Testament; put it into M’Donald’s hands, and desired him to say after him. M’Donald said his name was John Smith, and repeated after Baines, who appeared to read from a paper.

控方律师陈述道，去年七月，约翰·麦克唐纳和另一名眼线被派往哈利法克斯（由曼彻斯特非常活跃的警官纳丁先生派出），目的是查出起诉书中所控罪行的违规人员，他们于7月8日抵达那里。他们去了一家名叫克里斯平的客栈；在那里吃了午饭，随后去寻找住处，然后折返回来。在克里斯平客栈里，人们谈论了袭击卡特赖特面粉厂的情况。被告米尔内斯当时也在场，麦克唐纳便与他攀谈起来，谈论世道艰难。在谈论到面粉厂的谈话过程中，米尔内斯说他非常熟悉在那里被打死的那两个人，并谈到了指挥官像军官一样指挥若定。麦克唐纳表示，如果他们能拉他入伙，或者主持让他宣誓，他会和他们中的任何人一样积极。米尔内斯说他从一个士兵那里得到了许多空包弹，他曾被搜查过，但他跑掉了并躲了三个星期。米尔内斯告诉麦克唐纳，他可以让他被住在附近的一个老头子主持宣誓入伙。麦克唐纳表示同意。他们一起坐到天黑，然后在晚上十点左右一同去了贝恩斯家；当时所有的被告都在那里。米尔内斯告诉他们，麦克唐纳是个陌生人，但人不错，想成为兄弟。老贝恩斯随后说，他必须快点行动，因为他预计守夜巡逻队在十一点前就会过来。他没有再说什么，只是拿出一张纸和一本大约有《新约圣经》大小的书；把它放到麦克唐纳手中，要他跟着他念。麦克唐纳说自己的名字叫约翰·史密斯，跟着贝恩斯重复誓言，贝恩斯当时似乎是在对着一张纸念。

M’Donald does not recollect many words, but it was that he should never reveal any brother’s secrets, either by signs or words; and if any traitors rose up against them, they were to be put to death: and repeated much more than he could recolleot, and kissed the book. Prisoners were all sitting down imtil the oath was administered, when they all stood up. Zachary Baines, the boy, stood with his back against the door, to keep it shut. M’Donald himself was afraid, lest the watch and ward should come; he however sat down, and said he would pay for something to drink for them. Old Baines said he expected the watch and ward, and they had better go; that one of the neighbours had reported to the magistrates, and he was afraid of the watch and ward All the prisoners, except old Baines and the boy, went with M’Donald to the Crispin; Duckworth left them at the door; the said three went in. Gossling (the said police assistant) came in immediately after, and they sat drinking together. M’Donald told Gossling in the presence of the three prisoners then in the house, that he had been “twisted in,” and they said, “Yes he had;” and Milnes said he had introduced him. A day or two afterwards they met again, when M’Donald told Gossling, “That is the old man that .twisted ‘ me in.” Baines said he had, and put his hand up and said they must be cautious where they said that; hush! his eyes had been opened a matter of three-and-twentv years. This was confirmed by Gossling.

麦克唐纳记不清许多词，但大意是他永远不应该通过手势或言语泄露任何兄弟的秘密；如果有任何叛徒起来反对他们，他们应被处死。他还重复了许多他记不起来的内容，并吻了那本书。在主持宣誓之前，被告们都坐着，宣誓时他们全部站了起来。那个叫扎卡里·贝恩斯的男孩背靠着门站着，以防门被推开。麦克唐纳自己当时很害怕，担心守夜巡逻队会来；但他还是坐了下来，说他会请大家喝一杯。老贝恩斯说他预计守夜巡逻队快来了，他们最好还是离开；说有个邻居已经向治安官举报了，他害怕巡逻队。除了老贝恩斯和那男孩之外，所有的被告都和麦克唐纳一起去了克里斯平客栈；达克沃斯在门口离开了他们；其余三人进了店。警察助手戈斯林随后也进来了，他们坐在一起喝酒。在当时在店内的三名被告面前，麦克唐纳告诉戈斯林他已经被“拉入伙”了，被告们也说：“是的，他确实入伙了。”米尔内斯说是由他引荐的。一两天后他们再次相遇，麦克唐纳对戈斯林说：“就是那个老头子帮我主持入伙誓言的。”贝恩斯承认了，他举起手说，他们说这话时必须小心；嘘！他的双眼已经看清了大约二十三年。这一点得到了戈斯林的证实。

Mr. Joseph Nadin, a police officer at Manchester, stated that the two witnesses who had been examined on the part of the prosecution, had been employed by him to go to Halifax and other places, for the purpose of detecting persons in the habit of administering illegal oaths.

曼彻斯特的警官约瑟夫·纳丁先生陈述说，接受控方质询的两名证人均是由他雇佣去往哈利法克斯及其他地方的，目的是为了侦查习惯于主持非法宣誓的人员。

On the iiart of the prisoners, separate alibi were set forth for each of them, supported by witnesses apparently unconnected with each other.

被告方则分别为他们每个人提出了单独的不在场证明，并由显然互不相识的证人们提供证言。

John Thomas, a master shoemaker, and Thomas Cockroft, his apprentice, deposed that John Baines, the younger, was working at his shop at Luddenden, on the 8th of July, the day sworn to by John McDonald, and that he remained all night at their house. Luddenden is about four miles from Halifax.

鞋匠师傅约翰·托马斯和他的学徒托马斯·考克罗夫特宣誓作证，在约翰·麦克唐纳宣誓作证的7月8日那天，小约翰·贝恩斯正在他位于卢登登的作坊里工作，并且整晚都呆在他们家。卢登登距离哈利法克斯大约四英里。

William Longbottom, who lives at Outlane, six miles from Halifax, had occasion to come to Halifax on some business, and remained there over the night of the 8th of July, where he slept at the prisoner s (George Duckworth’s) father’s. On the night of the 8th of July was with the prisoner, George Duckworth, the whole of the evening, and could undertake to swear that he wae not at the house of John Baines the elder, that night.

住在奥特兰（距离哈利法克斯六英里）的威廉·朗博特姆因办一些事需要前往哈利法克斯，并在7月8日晚上留宿在那里，睡在被告乔治·达克沃斯的父亲家。在7月8日晚上，他整晚都和被告乔治·达克沃斯在一起，并且可以发誓乔治当晚绝不在老约翰·贝恩斯家里。

William Duckworth spoke to the fact of the last witness being at his house on the 8th of July, and remaining there all night.

威廉·达克沃斯证实了上一位证人于7月8日确实在他家，并留在那里过夜的事实。

Thomas Elwall is son-in-law of John Baines, the elder; is a private in the 33rd regiment; was at his house on the 8th of July last; went about eight o’clock and remained until twelve at night, and during that period no person came into the house except Zachary Baines, his brother; being asked how he could amuse the old man so long, he said he had been in the East Indies, and though he had often told the story before, the old man liked to hear it again and again.

托马斯·埃尔沃尔是老约翰·贝恩斯的女婿；是第33步兵团的列兵；去年7月8日他曾去老人家；大约八点去，一直呆到晚上十二点。在此期间，除了老人的儿子扎卡里·贝恩斯之外，没有任何人进过屋子。当被问及他怎么能陪老人家聊这么久时，他说他曾去过东印度群岛，虽然他以前经常讲那里的经历，但老人家就是喜欢听了一遍又一遍。

Hannah Crowther, of Halifax, stated that she saw Blakeborough on the 5th of July last, and it was proposed to go to Saddleworth on the 8th of July, to his brother’s; that they went on the day appointed, in the morning, and remained there ten days. Witness paid ninepence a day during the time she was there; paid between eleven and twelve shillings when she came away, to the prisoner’s brother. Witness is generally employed in burling cloth.

哈利法克斯的汉娜·克劳瑟陈述说，她于去年7月5日见到了布莱克伯勒，并商定在7月8日去萨德尔沃思他兄弟家。他们于约定好的那天早上出发，并在那里呆了十天。证人在那里的时间里每天支付九便士；离开时她向被告的兄弟支付了十一至十二先令。证人通常从事呢绒修整理毛的工作。

John Blakeborough stated the facts as deposed to by the List witness, as to his brother coming to his house on the 8th of July; that he remained there ten dayfe. The last witness, Hannah Crowther, was there; she had money to pay for her board, but she did not pay him any.

约翰·布莱克伯勒陈述了与上一位证人相同的叙述，证实他兄弟于7月8日来到他家，并在那里呆了十天。上一位证人汉娜·克劳瑟当时确实在那儿；她有钱支付伙食费，但她没有付给他任何钱。

After a charge from the Judge, the Jury retired for some time and then returned into court, and found a verdict of Guilty against all the prisoners, except Zachary Baines, who was acquitted.

在听取了法官的训诫后，陪审团退庭商议了一段时间，随后返回法庭，裁定除被宣告无罪释放的扎卡里·贝恩斯外，其余所有被告均有罪。

A smile lighted up for an instant the wrinkled face of old John Baines as he heard that his son Zachary was acquitted, and then he turned with a defiant air towards the Bench to hear his own sentence. The Judge cast an almost sympathetic glance at the stern old democrat, who had all his life suffered for the people’s cause, and who now faced him with unblanched cheek to hear what he knew would be his final doom. There was a moment’s dead silence in the court, and then the Judge slightly waved his hand as a signal for the removal of the prisoners. He would consider his sentence.

当听到儿子扎卡里被宣判无罪时，老约翰·贝恩斯布满皱纹的脸上闪过一丝笑意，随后他带着挑衅的神气转向法官席，准备听取自己的判决。法官向这位坚忍的老民主主义者投去了几乎充满同情的一瞥，此人一生都在为平民百姓的事业受苦，而如今他面不改色地迎向法官，准备听取他心知肚明的最终判决。法庭内出现了片刻死一般的寂静，随后法官微微挥了挥手，示意将犯人带离法庭。他将考虑他的量刑。

Scarcely was the dock clear, when the voice of the cryer of the court was heard, and one of the names was that which Rayner longed, yet feared to hear, “Job Hey, John Hill, Wm. Hartley,” rang through the court, and soon the three prisoners stood in front of the dock—the two first pale even to the lips, but the third wore his usual air of serious abstraction, which never for an instant left his face during the proceedings. Rayner gazed upon the face of his old friend with intense pity, and felt thankful that his daughter was not able to be present. Though only just turned forty, he looked as bowed and withered as one who had reached the full term allotted to man by the psalmist.

被告席刚空出来，法庭宣达员的声音便响了起来，其中一个名字正是雷纳既渴望听到又害怕听到的：“乔布·海伊、约翰·希尔、威廉·哈特利”的声音在法庭内回荡。不久，三名被告便站在了被告席前——前两人脸色苍白得连嘴唇都没有血色，但第三个人依然戴着他平日里那种严肃、若思的神情，在审判过程中他的脸上从未有一刻失去过这种神色。雷纳怀着极度的怜悯注视着这位老朋友的面庞，并为他的女儿玛丽无法到场感到庆幸。尽管他才刚满四十岁，但他看起来却如同圣诗作者所说的那样，已是弓腰驼背、油尽灯枯。

Mr. Park stated that the offence with which the three prisoners was charged, was connected with the disturbances in the West Riding. The obtaining of arms was one of the most prominent and alarming features attending that system of terror and outrage which had been carried to so great an extent. The crime of burglary was a capital one ,and to constitute this offence there must be a breaking into the house in the night-time, with an intent to steal; but as he had occasion to state to a jury on a former occasion, it would be the same thing in point of law, if the occupier of the house was by threats and intimidation compelled to open it; nor was it material whether the property was taken by the persons so entering the house, or delivered to them through fear.

帕克先生陈述道，三名被告所受的指控与西区的动乱有关。获取武器是那套已发展到极其严重程度的恐怖与暴行体系中最突出、最令人震惊的特征之一。夜盗罪是死罪，要构成这项罪行，必须是在夜间破门进入住宅并怀有盗窃意图。但正如他以前向陪审团陈述过的那样，在法律层面上，如果房屋的占有者因受到威胁和恐吓而被迫开门，其性质也是完全一样的；无论财产是进入房屋的人亲自动手拿走的，还是房主因害怕而亲手交给他们的，都不影响定罪。

George Haigh lives at Copley Gate, in Skircoat; in August last, he had a person lived with him of the name of Tillotson. On the night of the last Saturday in August he heard a loud rapping at the door; on hearing the noise he got up, and went to the landing place at the topi of the stairs; heard a loud rapping at the door, as if with the butt end of a gun. Witness then heard the voices of several persons at the door. The first thing he heard, was, “Your arms! your arms” Witness then cried, “Holla, holla! what do you want?” and was answered by one of the party “General Ludd, my master, has sent me for the arms you have.” Witness said to this demand, “I have nothing of the kind, for God’s sake ‘go home.” They then began firing: there was a continual noise, occasioned, as he supposed, by beating against the door in the porch, which reverberated the sound. Witness proceeded to state, that after some altercation with them, in which they insisted that he had two guns and four pistols, his apprentice said to him, “Master, you had better give them up, or they will shoot us,” on which he consented that he followed him into the house; they had guns with them, having again threatened to shoot him, if he did not give them the pistol. When the pistol was delivered to them, they told him if his master did should give them the gun. ‘Tillotson took it to the door to them. Witness never saw anything of the party; heard them say, “Your arms, your arms! be quick or we will shoot you;” the voice then seemed to proceed from the kitchen.

乔治·黑格住在斯基科特的科普利关卡；去年八月，有个名叫蒂洛森的人与他住在一起。八月最后一个星期六的深夜，他听到大门传来一阵剧烈的敲门声；听到动静后，他爬起床走到楼梯口的缓步台上；听见敲门声沉重，仿佛是用枪托砸门的声音。证人随后听见门口传来几个人的说话声。他听到的第一句话是：“交出你们的武器！交出你们的武器！”证人接着大喊：“喂，喂！你们要干什么？”对方中有人回答：“我的主人卢德将军派我来拿走你们所有的武器。”证人对这个要求回答说：“我没有这类东西，看在上帝的份上，回家去吧。”他们接着便开始朝屋里开枪：枪声和嘈杂声连成一片，他认为这是由于他们撞击门廊的门时发出的回音。证人继续陈述说，在与他们进行了一番口角后，对方坚称他有两把枪和四把手枪，他的徒弟对他说：“师傅，您最好还是把枪交出去吧，否则他们会朝我们开枪的。”于是他同意了，徒弟跟着他进了屋。对方带了枪，并再次威胁说如果他不把手枪交出来就开枪打死他。当手枪交到他们手上时，他们对徒弟说如果他师傅不交，就应该把猎枪交给他们。蒂洛森把枪拿到门口给了他们。证人从未看清这伙人的长相；只听到他们说：“你们的武器，你们的武器！快点，否则我们开枪了！”那声音随后似乎是从厨房里传出来的。

John Tilloteon lived with the last witness in August last; remembers some persons coming to their house in the night, but does not remember the day of the month; heard a great knocking at the door; witness then declared the circumstances stated by the last witness, as to the terms in which the demand was made for arms, but further stated that the people on the outside of the door said, if the door was not immediately opened, they would break into the house. His master told him he must get up, open the door, and give them the gun. When witness opened the door, some persons ran away from it to the corner of the house, where they were joined by some other persons; when they returned, they asked for guns and pistols; witness gave them the gun before they came into the house; witness remained within the door. They enquired if the gun was fireable, witness said it was; the ramrod being wanting, they told him if he did not find it immediately, they would shoot him; witness said he could not find it, on which they said there was another pistol, which they must have, and if they did not sell his milk among his neighbours, they would visit him again, with instant death. They took away with them a top-coat which belonged to him, but the coat was left at a farmhouse for him, and was returned next morning. Found the butt-end of a gun next morning near the door. The time of the attack was a little after twelve o’clock at night.

约翰·蒂洛森去年八月与上一位证人同住；记得一天夜里有人来到他们家，但不记得是哪一天；听见剧烈的敲门声；证人随后证实了上一位证人陈述的索要武器的具体措词，但进一步表示，门外的人说如果门不立刻打开，他们就要强行破门而入。他师傅告诉他他必须起床开门，把枪给他们。当证人打开门时，有几个人从门前跑向屋角，并在那里与其他人会合。当他们返回时，他们索要枪支和手枪；证人在他们进屋前就把猎枪递给了他们；证人一直呆在门内。他们询问这把枪能否开火，证人说可以；由于缺少通条，他们告诉他如果不能立刻找到，他们就开枪打死他；证人说他找不到，于是他们说还有一把手枪，他们也必须拿到，并且说如果他不在邻里之间卖牛奶，他们会再次找上门来立刻要了他的命。他们顺手拿走了一件属于他的外套，但那件外套后来被留在一个农舍里转交给他，并在第二天早上被送了回来。第二天早上，他在门附近发现了一个废弃的枪托。袭击发生的时间是半夜十二点刚过。

Cross-examined by Mr. Williams: They were not more than two or three minutes in the house, it was dark at the time. Neither of the two last witnesses spoke to the persons of any of the prisoners.

威廉姆斯先生对其进行了盘问：他们在屋里呆了不过两三分钟，当时天很黑。前两位证人均无法认出被告席上的任何人。

Joseph Carter (the informer) lives at North Green, in Greetland; knows the three prisoners; was in their company the latter end of August. Witness knows George Haigh; himself and the three prisoners went to various plaoes, and among others to George Haigh’s; the three prisoners at the bar were there; some of the party went to the kitchen door, and others to the front door; they knocked very hard, and demanded arms. Witness then stated the circumstances related to by former witnesses, previous to their entry into the house. When Tillotson delivered them the pistol, they then told him if his master did not sell his milk to his neighbours at twopence a quart they would visit him again. One of the party took a topcoat, and threw it on Job Hey’s arm, and he carried it from the house; after some time he enquired which of the party it belonged to, and finding that it did not belong to any of them, he said he would not have anything to do with it, and he left it at a farmhouse to be returned to Haigh’s. Job Hey took the gun into his possession, and another of the prisoners took the pistol. The gun was carried into North Dale. Job Hey had a gun when they went to Haigh’s house, with which he knocked at the kitchen door, and broke it, a part of the stock was left behind; there were no firearms discharged; he struck the door very hard. The gun that was broken had been got that night.

告密者约瑟夫·卡特住在格里特兰的北绿地；认识这三名被告；八月底曾与他们同行。证人知道乔治·黑格；他本人和三名被告去了许多地方，其中就包括乔治·黑格家。被告席上的三名被告当时都在场；他们中有人去了厨房门，有人去了前门；他们重重地敲门，索要武器。证人随后陈述了前几位证人所提到的在进入屋子前的情况。当蒂洛森把手枪交出来时，他们告诉他，如果他师傅不以每夸脱两便士的价格把牛奶卖给邻居，他们会再次找上门来。那伙人中的一个拿了一件大衣搭在乔布·海伊的胳膊上，海伊把它带离了那栋房子。过了一会儿，海伊询问这件外套属于谁，发现不属于他们中的任何人，便说他不想惹上这麻烦，把它留在一个农舍里退还给黑格家。乔布·海伊把枪收为己有，另一名被告拿了手枪。那把枪被带到了北山谷。当他们去黑格家时，乔布·海伊带了一把猎枪，他用枪撞击厨房的门并把门砸坏了，一小段枪托留在了现场。当时没有开火，他非常用力地撞击了门。那把砸坏的猎枪是当晚才到手的。

Cross-examined by Mr. Williams: He is a cotton spinner and came to York from the House of Correction at Wakefield; got home between one and two in the morning; did not get drunk that night, it was another night, had been regularly employed in stealing arms for six weeks or a month; was taken up on a Saturday night in December. Witness afterwards corrected himself, and said, that enquiries having been made after him, he surrendered himself up.

威廉姆斯先生对其进行了盘问：他是一名棉纺工，是从韦克菲尔德的惩教所押解到约克的；案发当晚在凌晨一两点之间回到家；当晚他没有喝醉，那是另一个晚上喝醉的。他曾在六周或一个月的时间里经常参与偷盗武器。他在十二月的一个星期六晚上被捕。证人随后纠正了说法，说由于有人四处搜查他，他便自首了。

Thomas Clark is a serjeant in the Suffolk Militia; there is a party stationed at Elland; apprehended Job Hey at his house in North Dean. On searching his house he found 3ilbs. of gunpowder. Job Hey told him he had had it sixteen years, but the gunpowder was fresh, and could not have been kept so long. In consequence of directions from Job Hey, he found a pistol concealed between the chimney and the roof.

托马斯·克拉克是萨福克郡民兵团的军士；有一支队伍驻扎在埃兰；他在北迪安的家中逮捕了乔布·海伊。在搜查他的房子时，他发现了3磅火药。乔布·海伊告诉他这些火药他已经存了十六年了，但那些火药非常新鲜，根本不可能保存这么久。根据乔布·海伊的交代，他在烟囱和屋顶之间发现了一把藏匿的手枪。

The examination taken before Mr. Radcliffe was then proved and read. Job Hey says, “I was there, at George Haigh’s;” and John Hill says, “I was there, but there was never any gun fired.” William Hartley, being charged with a felony, says, “I was there, but I had no arms, nor did I make any demand for any.” The examination contained an admission of other depredations, but only those parts were read which applied to the present charge.

随后法庭宣读了在拉德克利夫先生面前进行的审讯记录。乔布·海伊说：“我当时在乔治·黑格家。”约翰·希尔说：“我当时在场，但从来没有开过枪。”被控犯有重罪的威廉·哈特利说：“我当时在场，但我没有带武器，也没有索要过任何东西。”审讯记录中还包含对其他破坏活动的承认，但法庭只宣读了适用于本项指控的部分。

Two witnesses were called to speak to the character of the prisoners, who represented them as honest, industrious men.

法庭传唤了两名证人就犯人们的品行作证，他们形容被告们是诚实、勤劳的人。

Mr. Justice Le Blanc, after commenting upon the evidence, and stating the law to the jury, as laid down by the counsel for the crown, said, that character, which could not weigh much except in case of doubt, had less weight than usual in the present temper and discontent which had manifested itself in those districts, and where men, who would have shrunk back from the proposal of an ordinary robbery, engaged with alacrity in those depredations.

勒布兰克法官在对证据发表评论并向陪审团阐明了控方所主张的法律条款后表示，品行证言除了在证据存疑的案子中能起到一定分量外，在目前这些地区所表现出的普遍对立和不满情绪下，其分量比以往更轻。因为在这些地方，许多原本对普通抢劫提议退避三舍的人，却十分积极地参与到了这些劫掠破坏活动中。

The Jury, without retiring, found the prisoners— Guilty.

陪审团未经退庭商议，当场裁定被告：有罪。

The prisoners received sentence of death on the following day, and were among the unhappy prisoners left for execution.

被告们于次日被判处死刑，他们也名列等待执行死刑的不幸囚犯名单之中。
<!-- Part 037 end -->

<!-- Part 038 start -->
## 第二十七章 罗福兹暴动案审判

> “我们发现得太迟，
> 既无信义，也无感恩，更无友善的信任；
> 在有罪之人之间，
> 任何契约的约束力都无法存续。”
> *——* **布鲁克**。

1813年1月19日，星期六。道尔顿的詹姆斯·海格（21岁）、哈德斯菲尔德的乔纳森·迪恩（30岁）、哈德斯菲尔德的约翰·奥格登（28岁）、洛克伍德的詹姆斯·布鲁克（26岁）、同地的约翰·布鲁克（22岁）、同地的托马斯·布鲁克（32岁）、朗罗伊德桥的约翰·沃克（31岁）以及利弗塞奇的约翰·赫斯特（28岁）被起诉，罪名是他们与乔治·梅勒、威廉·索普、托马斯·史密斯以及一百多名陪审团成员不知名的其他人一起，于4月11日夜间进行暴动集会，并着手拆毁一座由威廉·卡特赖特先生占用、位于利弗塞奇教区罗福兹的水力磨坊。

起诉书中提到的最后三人在前一天因谋杀霍斯福尔先生而被处决。

被告人均辩称——无罪。

随后，组成陪审团的以下绅士宣誓就职：艾萨克·牛顿、约翰·米克尔斯韦特、戈德弗雷·帕克、威廉·帕克、亨利·波普尔韦尔、杰瓦斯·西顿、克里斯托弗·史密斯、罗伯特·斯塔宾、理查德·托蒂、托马斯·图塔尔、理查德·沃丁顿、亨利·威尔金森。

公诉方律师：帕克先生、托平先生、霍尔罗伊德先生和理查德森先生。

被告辩护律师：布鲁厄姆先生、哈洛克先生和威廉姆斯先生。

陪审团要求提供一份被告人名单，名单已递交给他们。

公诉方和被告方的所有证人都被要求退庭。

帕克先生表示，他必须频繁提及那些已不在人世的不幸之人的名字，这并非因为他想对他们说些刻薄的话，而是因为这是不可避免的。

理查德森先生宣读了起诉书，帕克先生陈述了起诉方的案情，并传唤了以下证人：——

威廉·卡特赖特先生，由托平先生询问。他陈述说，在去年4月11日，他占有位于西区利弗塞奇镇罗福兹的一座磨坊。已占有该磨坊近三年。这是一座水力磨坊，专为通过机器整理布料而建造。在4月11日之前，他一直担心或者说料到磨坊会遭到袭击，因此，根据这一预期，他采取了他认为最适合保障其安全和防护的措施。在袭击发生前的六周里，他一直睡在磨坊里，并准备了火枪和弹药，在他的工人中，有几个人在袭击发生前的一周里也睡在磨坊里。证人在磨坊里设有床铺，他自己睡在账房里。

在去年4月11日（星期六），他在磨坊里除了自己之外，还有五名士兵和四名他自己的雇员。证人在十二点二十五分上床睡觉；一刻钟后，他听到狗狂吠起来；这只狗在底层，是专门为了在夜间有人靠近时发出警报而安置在那里的。他起床时以为狗是报了假警，因为他以为第一声警报应该来自大楼外面的守卫。当他打开账房门时，他震惊地听到密集的火枪射击声，并伴随着底层窗户的剧烈破碎声；破碎声非常大：与此同时，有人开始猛烈锤击大门，部分袭击者绕到了大楼末端的另一扇门。大楼的一侧由一个水池保护，那一侧只有一条狭窄的步道。卡特赖特先生接着陈述说，他们立刻拿起前一天晚上堆放好的武器；他们根本来不及穿衣服，他也没想过穿衣服，而是立刻开始了猛烈的还击。

屋顶上装有一口钟，用于向驻扎在附近的骑兵小分队报警。这口钟立即被敲响，但不幸的是绳子断了。他们通过射击孔开火，这些孔相对于大楼内部是倾斜的，但可以控制磨坊的前方。门外的人持续有规律地射击了相当长的时间。他继续听到对大门的极其剧烈的撞击声和锤击声，偶尔能听到大声的叫喊：“加把劲，小伙子们！”、“冲进去！”、“你们进去了吗？”、“紧跟上！”、“该死的钟，去把它弄哑！”在第一次敲钟时，钟绳几乎立刻就断了，但他认为继续报警至关重要，因此命令两个人爬上屋顶去敲钟。卡特赖特先生说，他清晰地听到这样的话：“冲进去，小伙子们！”、“该死的，把他们一个个杀光！”人数似乎相当多。双方持续不断地对射了一段时间，但从对方射击的枪声来看，他估计袭击持续了长达二十分钟。外面的枪声减弱后，他们也减少了内部的射击，以避免流血。接着，他听到一侧传来混乱的警报声，似乎是有人试图抬走伤员。人们在离开时似乎分成了两路，但最终都通往哈德斯菲尔德。如果不是因为考虑到在有人来见证大楼和门窗的状况之前开门是不明智的，证人在他们的同伴离开现场后，是绝不会有片刻迟疑去救治伤员的；在许多人对他抱有敌意的心态下，他认为这是一种必要的预防措施。第一个来的人是库克希尔先生，随后门被打开了。他们发现了两名伤员，并在当时极其忙乱和混乱的情况下尽可能地对他们进行了照顾。卡特赖特先生随后开始描述袭击后磨坊的状况；底层的窗户除了三百块玻璃中的九块外，全部被打碎，窗户的木工部分受损严重，以至于完全无法使用，所有的窗框都必须拆除。其中一扇门几乎字面意义上被劈成了碎片，上面破了洞，人手都可以穿过去；在劈门时，他们似乎有时击中了门周围的石墙。另一扇门没有受损。上层的窗户也受到了相当大的损坏。这座石质建筑遭到了多次射击，弹痕依然清晰可见。第二天早上，他自己和工人们发现了许多用于袭击的工具。哈洛克先生只是问了几个问题，以确定卡特赖特先生是否是该磨坊的唯一占用者，他作了肯定的回答。卡特赖特先生说，他无法指认任何参与袭击的个人。在回答法官提问时，他陈述说底层窗户的所有窗框都受损严重，不得不全部拆除。卡特赖特先生展示了一个大袋子，里面装满了斧头、大木槌、铁锤、面具和袭击中使用的其他破坏工具，还有一杆火枪的枪托，以及在磨坊水坝中发现的一顶男士帽子。

接下来传唤了詹姆斯·桑兹，他证实袭击后的第二天早上，在卡特赖特先生展示的袋子里的大部分物品都是被他发现的。

詹姆斯·威尔金森在袭击当天的早上五点左右在磨坊水坝里发现了一个袋子，证人在磨坊附近捡到了卡特赖特先生展示的部分工具，其中一些是在磨坊内部发现的。

威廉·霍尔（线人）陈述说，他是一名剪绒工，记得卡特赖特先生的磨坊遭到袭击的那个星期六；证人在哈德斯菲尔德附近的朗罗伊德桥的约翰·伍德工场工作，被告人中没有人在那里工作；他认识剪绒工约书亚·迪金森，中午左右在约翰·伍德的店铺见过他；索登当时也在场，他是来送火药和弹丸的；被告人均不在场；他带来了很多火药（大约一品脱）和很多弹丸；火药是用纸包着的，弹丸装在一个小袋子里；他还带来了两三发子弹；给了他做什么的指示，由于这些指示，他去了属于乔治·阿米蒂奇爵士的一块田地；他与史密斯和乔治·戴森一起去，并赶上了洛克伍德的乔治·布鲁克；大约十点钟到达乔治爵士的田地，到达时发现聚集了四五十人；在那里待了将近一个小时，其间又有许多其他人加入了他们；无法说出具体人数，但显然有一百多人。证人陈述说，在他们离开田地之前，他们点了人名，不是点名字，而是点编号，每个人在听到自己的编号被叫到时进行回答；

证人是7号；有一个人负责整顿他们的秩序。他们被分成了小队；证人在手枪队。梅勒和索普是将他们排成队列的人；手枪队有两个小队；还有一个火枪队排在最前面行进，排成两列，每排十人；证人在手枪队；乔治·里格和证人奉命走在最后，督促队伍前行，并确保没有人掉头回去。他们排成一列穿过哈茨黑德荒原，并以这种方式前往罗福兹。证人说，他们在一个被称为“哑塔”（Dumb-steeple）的地方集结。证人说，有拿斧头的人，还有些人拿着木棍，还有些人什么也没拿；也有人拿着铁锤和大木槌。当他们到达罗福兹时，他们停了下来，排成每排十三人的横队；梅勒组成了火枪队，索普组成了下一队。当证人被要求看着被告席并指出那里的所有人时，他指认了詹姆斯·海格、乔纳森·迪恩、约翰·奥格登、詹姆斯·布鲁克、约翰·布鲁克、约翰·沃克和约翰·赫斯特；他没有在那里看到托马斯·布鲁克。他们排成一列，然后向磨坊前进。磨坊内部传来了密集的射击声；证人是最后一批人之一，当他走到磨坊跟前时，他们正在砸碎窗户和门；听到梅勒喊道：“门开了！”、“朝那口钟射击！”；听到有人喊道：“有人中弹了！”，看见一个人躺在地上，他不认识那个人。证人向磨坊里开了两次枪。双方的射击持续了相当长的时间。前门被劈开了，但没有被打开。证人只看到地上躺着一个人；射击停止后，他们尽可能快地逃跑了；他涉过小溪（beck），向海敦（Hightown）的方向走去。他赶上了詹姆斯·戴森。没有看到任何被告人离开磨坊。没有看到任何没戴帽子的人。托马斯·布鲁克带了一顶帽子到约翰·伍德的工场，告诉证人如果可以的话把帽子送到它所属的地方，并让他去找乔治·梅勒，说梅勒会告诉他这顶帽子是谁的。梅勒告诉证人，他直到到达海敦才知道自己身在何处，并且他顺路拜访了塞缪尔·内勒。证人说他不记得在路上停下来过，也不记得同行的任何人停下来过，也没听到他们在路上要过任何东西。他们集结的乔治·阿米蒂奇爵士的田地距离卡特赖特先生的磨坊大约三英里。

由威廉姆斯先生交叉质询：证人说，那晚的视线还算不错，不是很黑，他在适度的距离内，比如一两码，就能认出一个人。他说他在乔治爵士的田里看到了被告人；不知道在此之后是否还见过他们，他看到他们站在一起，没有排队，并且在他们到达这块被指定为集结地的田地之前没有见过他们。在袭击发生之前，证人就认识梅勒和索普；证人说他在罗宾逊开的酒馆里见过海格，但并没有要求一个名叫贝里的人或任何其他人指向海格，也从未说过他不认识海格；他以前就认识海格；能通过声音辨认出那里的很多人；在乔治·阿米蒂奇爵士的田里看到了他所说的那几个人；在他们停下来的地方没有看到他们。

在接受重新质询时，他说，因为他被要求确保没有人掉头回去，所以他可以说只有两个人离开了队伍，他们放这两个人走是因为他们生病了，留下来也没用。
<!-- Part 038 end -->

<!-- Part 039 start -->
## 第二十八章 罗福兹暴动案审判续

> 罪恶是悲伤之源；它是恶魔——
> 尾随在我们身后的复仇恶魔，
> 带着皮鞭与毒刺。——
> *——* **罗威**。

下一位被传唤的证人是约瑟夫·德雷克，由托平先生质询。他说他是一名整理布料工，在卡特赖特先生的磨坊遭到袭击时在约翰·德雷克处工作。他与约翰·沃克和乔纳森·迪恩一起从乔纳森·迪恩的家中出发。他在晚上十点左右动身。他与他们相识已有一段时间。他们要在乔治·阿米蒂奇爵士的一块田地里会合。在去往那里的路上，他们赶上了许多去往同一个地方的人，但没有赶上任何被告人。当他们到达会合地点时，他们发现聚集了相当多的人，大约有一百三十到一百五十人。他在乔治爵士的田里没有看到任何被告人。他们被按编号点名，并两个两个排好。他们还被编成小队；说不清是谁给他们编队的。他们中有很多人带有武器。证人在手枪队：不知道一共有多少个队。有火枪队、手枪队，还有斧头队。当他们被整队完毕后，他们便向卡特赖特先生的磨坊行进。证人没有去到磨坊，而是在距离磨坊大约六十码的地方停了下来。他们离开乔治·阿米蒂奇制造的田地后，他就再也没见过被告人迪恩，但也没看到他离开。证人在他们停下的地方听到了密集的射击声。队伍中的许多人都留在了后面。证人在半路上带了一支手枪，但他们停下时他身上没有手枪；那时他没有任何武器。主力队伍向磨坊前进；枪声很大；由于射击的喧闹声，听不到打破窗户的声音；射击持续了一刻钟到二十分钟。迪恩随身带了一把锤子，是应约翰·沃克的邀请前去的，约翰·沃克随身带了一支手枪，穿着一件工作服；他不认识詹姆斯·海格；他认识托马斯·布鲁克，在他们返回时在海敦第一次见到他。队伍朝不同的方向散去。证人朝海敦走去；他见到托马斯·布鲁克时，布鲁克身上什么也没拿；他以前见过他，他的衣服湿透了。证人记得布鲁克说他掉进过磨坊水坝里，而且他没戴帽子。乔治·梅勒和他在一起，他们停在塞缪尔·内勒家，在那里为托马斯·布鲁克借了一顶帽子。梅勒是去借帽子的人，并与他们同行；他们在克利夫顿附近的另一个地方停下，在那里要了一些松饼和水；一位妇女从窗口递给他们一些。帽子是塞缪尔·内勒的妻子递给他们的。证人认识约翰·奥格登（另一名被告人）：在4月11日之前就认识他；袭击发生后在海敦遇到了他；之前没有见过他；他随身带了一支手枪，没有别的东西；他说他参与了对磨坊的袭击；在到达考克利夫（Cowcliffe）之前，他们与他分手了。

由布鲁厄姆先生交叉质询：他说，在他们在乔治·阿米蒂奇爵士的田里排成队列之前，夜色非常黑暗；那块田地也靠近一条占了相当大空间的小巷；它还靠近一条沿森林边缘延伸的小巷；他们在那里停留了一刻钟，当时非常黑。

本杰明·沃克（谋杀霍斯福尔先生的帮凶）陈述说，他是袭击当晚前往罗福兹磨坊的一员；他与乔治·梅勒、威廉·索普和托马斯·史密斯（已被处决的不幸之人）一起前往乔治·阿米蒂奇爵士的田地；他在那里没有看到任何被告人，在到达那里之前也没有见过他们；他是13号；他们被分成了不同的组。梅勒的小队是第一队，主要装备有枪支，下一队则装备有手枪。乔治·梅勒指挥第一队，索普指挥第二队。在从乔治·阿米蒂奇爵士的田地出发，介于该地和他们停下之处的途中，他看到了被告人乔纳森·迪恩；那位置几乎已经靠近磨坊；证人继续留在配备火枪的第一队；当时有密集的射击；证人开了枪。证人看到了受伤的布斯，他后来死了；他没有看到其他任何人受伤。证人在袭击结束后的次日早上大约六点钟，在乔纳森·迪恩自己的家里见到了他：他躺在床上，手在流血；他告诉证人他受伤了，但没有告诉他是怎么伤的；说是手指中弹受伤了，但证人没有与他进一步交谈。证人陈述说，他们在一个地方停下来为托马斯·布鲁克（被告人之一）借了一顶帽子；他们停下来的地方是海敦。托马斯·布鲁克告诉他，他在磨坊的引水渠丢了帽子；他说他掉进去了；他的衣服非常湿。在乔治爵士的围地和他们靠近磨坊停下之处之间，他看到了约翰·沃克；沃克随身带了一支手枪，此后过了一段时间他又见到了他。他在乔治·阿米蒂奇爵士的田里看到了詹姆斯·海格；以为他带了一把大木槌；在那里没有看到其他被告人。证人知道克利夫顿村；他们在那里得到了一些松饼，是一个妇女给他们的；他当时配备了一杆火枪和一支手枪；他把火枪给了瓦利。证人那天晚上戴了面具，其他一些人也戴了面具伪装。梅勒命令他烧掉面具，他照做了。被告辩护律师没有对该证人进行质询。

约书亚·索登陈述说，他是朗罗伊德桥约翰·伍德工场的工人；记得对卡特赖特磨坊的袭击。证人认识乔纳森·迪恩、约翰·沃克和布鲁克三兄弟。证人从未亲自从他们口中听说过卡特赖特磨坊里发生的事情；此后十个星期里都没见过他们；在下周初与约翰·沃克进行过谈话：谈话发生在约翰·伍德的店铺；他听到沃克说（并非针对证人说话），他有一支军用手枪；当时他正站在那儿朝窗户里看，一颗子弹穿过并击中了他的帽顶，他便把手伸进窗户，朝火光发出的地方开枪，并说：“就算我的手要一起废掉，我也下定决心要开枪。”之后就没有再说别的了。在袭击发生前，乔纳森·迪恩和约翰·沃克是最先提出效仿诺丁汉的砸机器体制的人，并与另一个人一起来亲自要求他们商讨、谋划并采取摧毁机器的计划。当时没有提到卡特赖特先生的磨坊；起初是以恳求的方式提出申请，但后来变成了威胁的方式。在他的听闻中，没有提到过对卡特赖特先生磨坊的袭击计划。证人说，他非但不同意这些行径，反而一直对此深恶痛绝。证人从未听任何一个布鲁克谈起过此事。

哈洛克先生：“既然你对这些行径深恶痛绝，你为什么没有立刻去告发呢？”

索登：“因为我没料到事情会发展到今天这个地步。”

哈洛克先生：“但当你发现事情已经发展到那个地步时，你为什么还不告发？你为什么要把这些暴行在心里隐瞒这么久？”

索登：“在那种情况下，任何一个和我有着同样精神的人都会像我这样做的。”

哈洛克先生：“请问，先生，你有什么样的精神？”

索登：“胆怯的精神。”

哈洛克先生：“但看来你最终胆怯地屈服了。你最后又是如何鼓起勇气进行揭露的呢？”

索登：“当我宣誓接受质询时，我必须说出真相，而不管后果如何。”

证人陈述说，他是从10月24日开始，才首次透露了他在证词中所说的那些情况。

玛丽·布鲁克在去年4月住在克利夫顿；记得罗福兹骚乱的那天晚上，有人来到门前要求买些面包；她起床后，通过一块破碎的窗玻璃，递给他们松饼和一罐水，他们给了她三便士。证人没有看清任何人的模样。

萨拉·内勒太太住在海敦的顶端，已婚；记得在卡特赖特磨坊遭到袭击的当晚，有人敲了她家的门，要求她借给一个男人一顶帽子，她照办了；她不认识任何来她家的人。

理查德·塔特索尔在莱普顿行医；记得4月12日星期日有人来到他家，并在随后的星期二再次见到了他；他仅在给其包扎伤口时见过他。证人后来在拉德克利夫先生处见到了这个人，并认出了他。证人随后被要求看着被告席并指认他（如果在场的话）。证人看着被告席并指认了詹姆斯·海格。詹姆斯·海格第一次来他家是在下午四点左右；他说他想包扎伤口。伤口在右肩后部；大约有一英寸深；它不是穿透伤，而是一个开放性伤口。他的衬衫上有血迹；伤口边缘有一些棉绒；伤口宽度大于深度，是一个相当大的伤口。海格说他来自道尔顿，伤口是被石头弄伤的。证人什么也没对他说，只是给他包扎了伤口；它看起来像是个擦伤，而且看起来确实可能是石头造成的。它看起来像是个新伤口；他在两端和中间缝合了伤口，总共缝了三针。海格没有透露自己的名字。法官问了几个问题，以确定伤口是穿透伤还是开放性伤口，但证人坚持声称这只是一个开放性伤口。

约书亚·卡尔潘住在佩尼斯顿格林一栋孤立的房子里；那里距离哈德斯菲尔德十四英里；他知道一个叫道尔顿的地方，距离他家大约十二英里。证人有一个住在道尔顿的名叫阿德隆的亲戚；去年4月见过他，他来到了他家；还有另一个人和他在一起；那是4月15日夜间十二点到一点之间。证人当时在床上。交谈一番后，他和妻子起床，让这两人躺下；他家里只有一张床，证人说与他亲戚在一起的那个人是詹姆斯·海格。证人以为海格说过他受了伤；但证人似乎极其不愿意说明过去发生的事。证人说他早上五点左右起床，吃过一点简单的早餐后，与他的亲戚阿德隆一起出发，前往住在威洛桥的阿德隆的母亲家，将海格留在他的屋里。他们拜访的目的，是为詹姆斯·海格去那里做准备。海格在证人家里一直待到第二天的下午。阿德隆的母亲住的地方距离他家大约一英里，距离道尔顿十三英里。被告辩护律师没有对该证人进行提问。

托马斯·阿特金森于4月23日前往詹姆斯·海格在道尔顿的家中实施逮捕；发现屋里没有人；有一些装有麦芽汁的酿酒桶，原本处于发酵状态，但现在发酵已经完全停止了。证人发现门锁着，就把门砸开了。由于得到了一些线索，他去了乡间外科医生塔特索尔那里，接着去了佩尼斯顿阿德隆母亲的住处，又从那里去了拉格比，再从拉格比去了梅思利，他在梅思利逮捕了詹姆斯·海格；从梅思利到道尔顿的直线距离是十九英里，但由于他绕道而行，路程要长得多。证人在他的姐夫（或妹夫）家里找到了他；他的肩膀受了伤。证人将他带到治安官面前；没有问他关于他是如何受伤的任何问题。证人陈述说，被告人的衬衫在拉德克利夫先生家被脱了下来，拉德克利夫先生给了他一件自己干净的衬衫。这件衬衫展示给戈登少校后，他表示他的意见是：衬衫上的破洞是被火枪弹丸穿透造成的；詹姆斯·海格听到了这个说法，但没有发表意见。这件衬衫已经被缝补过，被告人说是他妻子缝补的；衬衫在法庭上被出示了。

迈克尔·本特利先生陈述说，他记得去年4月见过被告人詹姆斯·海格；他是在证人自己的家中第一次见到海格的，并在那里给他刮了脸；那是他被捕的前一天——星期日；证人说他的肩膀似乎受伤了，他觉得是左肩；证人没有问关于他伤口的任何问题，但问了他是否是卢德分子中的一员，对此海格没有做任何回答。

事务律师阿里森先生被传唤以证实对乔纳森·迪恩的审讯记录，但由于似乎曾给迪恩透露过透露情况可能对他有利的预期，帕克先生表示他将放弃使用该记录。

约翰·赫斯特的自白书被证实后，被提交并进行了宣读，他在其中承认自己去了罗福兹，并听到了枪声。

芬妮·米尔斯太太认识被告人之一詹姆斯·布鲁克，并住在他附近；两家的大门只隔着一扇窗户。在卡特赖特先生的磨坊遭到袭击的次日早晨之后，证人说她没有看到什么特别的情况，但听到了大量的悄悄话。詹姆斯·布鲁克正在讲述一个非常悲伤的故事，这从他双手的动作中可以看出。有许多剪绒工进出那栋房子；他们是在他们工场干活的人。证人知道如果她进去他们就会停止谈话，所以便在后面停步，听到詹姆斯·布鲁克说：“这真是我这辈子听过的最阴森可怕的喧闹声了，在半英里外都能听到；我宁愿被饿死，也绝不想再参与这样的一场骚乱了。”

当被告人被要求进行辩护时，詹姆斯·海格和乔纳森·迪恩说他们无罪，但将辩护留给他们的律师。约翰·奥格登说他从未与他们在一起。詹姆斯·布鲁克说他没有参与此事。托马斯·布鲁克说他从未去过那个地方；约翰·布鲁克和约翰·赫斯特重复说他们无罪。
